=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a city divided into H \times W blocks in the north-south-east-west directions, and there is exactly one building in each block.
Specifically, in the block at the i-th row from the north (1\leq i\leq H) and the j-th column from the west (1\leq j\leq W) (hereafter referred to as block (i,j)), there is a building of F_{i,j} floors.
Takahashi has two ways of moving. If he is on the X-th floor (1\leq X\leq F_{i,j}) of the building in block (i,j), he can:

- Move up or down one floor within the same building using stairs. If X=1, he cannot move down; if X=F_{i,j}, he cannot move up.
- Choose a building with at least X floors in a cardinally adjacent block, and move to the X-th floor of that building using a (sky) walkway.

Here, two blocks (i,j) and (i',j') are cardinally adjacent if and only if \lvert i - i'\rvert + \lvert j - j'\rvert = 1.
You are given Q queries to be answered. The i-th query (1\leq i\leq Q) is the following.

Find the minimum possible number of times that Takahashi uses stairs to move from the Y_i-th floor of the building in block (A_i,B_i) to the Z_i-th floor of the building in block (C_i,D_i).
The count of times using stairs is incremented each time he moves up or down one floor, possibly multiple times within the same building. (For example, moving from the 1st floor to the 6th floor of a building counts as 5 uses of stairs.)
Note that he does not have to minimize the number of times he uses walkways.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
H W
F_{1,1} F_{1,2} \ldots F_{1,W}
F_{2,1} F_{2,2} \ldots F_{2,W}
\vdots
F_{H,1} F_{H,2} \ldots F_{H,W}
Q
A_1 B_1 Y_1 C_1 D_1 Z_1
A_2 B_2 Y_2 C_2 D_2 Z_2
\vdots
A_Q B_Q Y_Q C_Q D_Q Z_Q

Output

Print Q lines. The i-th line should contain the answer to the i-th query as an integer.

Constraints


- 1\leq H \leq 500
- 1\leq W \leq 500
- 1\leq F_{i,j} \leq 10^6
- 1\leq Q\leq 2\times 10^5
- 1\leq A_i,C_i\leq H
- 1\leq B_i,D_i\leq W
- 1\leq Y_i\leq F_{A_i,B_i}
- 1\leq Z_i\leq F_{C_i,D_i}
- (A_i,B_i,Y_i)\neq (C_i,D_i,Z_i)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 3
12 10 6
1 1 3
8 6 7
2
1 1 10 3 1 6
1 1 6 1 2 4

Sample Output 1

10
2

For the first query, for example, it is possible to move from the 10th floor of the building in block (1,1) to the 6th floor of the building in block (3,1) by using stairs a total of 10 times, in the following manner:

- Move from the 10th floor of the building in block (1,1) to the 10th floor of the building in block (1,2) via a walkway.
- Use stairs 4 times to go from the 10th floor down to the 6th floor of the building in block (1,2).
- Move from the 6th floor of the building in block (1,2) to the 6th floor of the building in block (1,3) via a walkway.
- Use stairs 3 times to go from the 6th floor down to the 3rd floor of the building in block (1,3).
- Move from the 3rd floor of the building in block (1,3) to the 3rd floor of the building in block (2,3) via a walkway.
- Move from the 3rd floor of the building in block (2,3) to the 3rd floor of the building in block (3,3) via a walkway.
- Use stairs 3 times to go from the 3rd floor up to the 6th floor of the building in block (3,3).
- Move from the 6th floor of the building in block (3,3) to the 6th floor of the building in block (3,2) via a walkway.
- Move from the 6th floor of the building in block (3,2) to the 6th floor of the building in block (3,1) via a walkway.

It is impossible to make this journey using at most 9 uses of stairs, so we output 10.
For the second query, if you first use a walkway to go to the building in block (1,2), and then use the stairs twice to go from the 6th floor down to the 4th floor, it is possible to move from the 6th floor of the building in block (1,1) to the 4th floor of the building in block (1,2) by using the stairs twice.

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's verify formula thoroughly with examples and edge cases.

Example: H=1,W=2, F=[5,5]. Query (1,1,4)->(1,2,2). M=5, min=2, cost=2. Route: walkway at floor2 from cell1 to cell2? Start at floor4, descend to2 (2), walk, end. yes.

Query (1,1,5)->(1,2,1). M=5, min=1, cost=4. Route descend to1 cost4, walk, end. Could also walk at5 then descend cost4. yes.

Query (1,1,5)->(1,2,5). M=5, cost=0. Walkway at5. yes.

If F=[5,1], M=1. Query (1,1,5)->(1,2,5). Formula |0|+2*(5-1)=8. Route: start floor5, descend to1 at cell1 cost4, walk to cell2 floor1, ascend to5 cost4 total8. Could walk? cell2 height1 so cannot at5. yes. Query (1,1,5)->(1,2,1): cost 4+2*(1-1)=4. Route descend to1 cost4 walk. yes. Query (1,1,2)->(1,2,1): min=1, M=1 cost1. Route descend to1 cost1. yes.

If path bottleneck M=3, y=5,z=4, cost=1+2*(4-3)=3. Route: start5 descend to3 cost2, traverse, ascend to4 cost1 total3. Alternative descend to4 at start cost1, traverse? Need path bottleneck 3 <4, cannot traverse at4. So no. Could traverse part at4 then descend at low cell? Need cross low cell at3, so at least 2 down +1 up =3. yes.

What about y=5,z=1, M=3 (>=min=1), cost=4. Route: start5 descend to1 cost4, traverse at1, end. Could also traverse at3: descend2, traverse, descend2? Actually end1, cost4. yes.

What about y=5,z=1, M=0? F>=1 always, so M>=1. no.

Now, is M(s,t) exactly max bottleneck path with vertex weights F. Need if path can include start/end cells multiple times; yes.

Potential issue: Walkway can move to adjacent building with at least X floors. If at floor X in building u, and adjacent v has F_v >= X, can move. This is equivalent to edge between u,v at level X. If path P has min F >= X, then all adjacent edges along P have both endpoints >=X, so can traverse. Conversely, if there is a walkway route at fixed floor X from s to t, the sequence of cells forms a path where every cell has F>=X. So X<=M(s,t). Good.

Now, can vertical moves happen in a building that is not on the horizontal path? No, vertical moves are at current building, which is on route. So cell path includes it.

Let's attempt formal lower bound:
Take any valid route. Let P be the sequence of buildings visited (including starts/ends and vertical/horizontal transitions). Let b = min_{v in P} F_v. Since every time route is at floor f in building v, f <= F_v. Thus all floors used in buildings with min? Actually b is min height among buildings visited; there is at least one visited building with height b. When at that building, the current floor f must be <= b. Therefore the route's floor level at some time is <= b. Let m = min(y,z). If b < m, then starting floor and ending floor are both > b (or one equals? If m > b, both y,z > b). The vertical coordinate starts at y, at some time reaches <=b, and ends at z. Total vertical variation (sum of absolute changes) is at least (y-b)+(z-b) = y+z-2b, because to go from y to <=b and then to z, regardless of extra, variation >= (y-b)+(z-b). If b >= m, then total variation at least |y-z| trivially. So cost >= |y-z| + 2 max(0, m-b). Since b <= M? Need show b <= M(s,t). The sequence P is a walk from s to t. It contains a simple path P' from s to t whose vertices are a subset of P. The bottleneck of P' is >= b? Wait if P' is subset, min F over P' >= min F over P = b. So M >= bottleneck(P') >= b. Thus b <= M. Therefore lower bound cost >= |y-z| + 2 max(0, m-M) (since max(0,m-b) >= max(0,m-M)? If b<=M, m-b >= m-M if m>b? Need if b<=M, then max(0,m-b) >= max(0,m-M)? Yes because m-b >= m-M, and if m-M negative then RHS 0, LHS nonnegative. Good). So lower bound.

Upper bound: choose a path P with bottleneck M. If M >= m, traverse at floor m: vertical from y to m at s cost |y-m|, horizontal along P at floor m (valid since m<=M), vertical from m to z at t cost |m-z|; total |y-z|. If M < m, traverse at floor M: vertical from y to M cost y-M, horizontal, vertical from M to z cost z-M; total y+z-2M = |y-z|+2(m-M). Valid because M<=F along P and M<=m<=y,z? Since M<m=min(y,z), yes M<y,z, so start/end buildings height >= y,z >M, vertical valid. Thus formula.

Good.

Now need compute M(s,t). Let's decide implementation approach.

Option B: Build Kruskal reconstruction tree (KRT) with sorted edges, then LCA.

Need LCA function:
```
def lca(u,v):
    if depth[u] < depth[v]: u,v = v,u
    diff = depth[u]-depth[v]
    bit=0
    while diff:
        if diff & 1:
            u = up[bit][u]
        diff >>= 1; bit += 1
    if u == v: return u
    for k in range(LOG-1, -1, -1):
        if up[k][u] != up[k][v]:
            u = up[k][u]; v = up[k][v]
    return up[0][u]
```

Edge generation with buckets:
```
maxF = max(F_flat)
head = [-1]*(maxF+1)
eu = [0]*E
ev = [0]*E
nxt = [0]*E
e=0
for i in range(H):
  base=i*W
  for j in range(W):
    idx=base+j
    if j+1<W:
      w = F[idx] if F[idx] < F[idx+1] else F[idx+1]
      eu[e]=idx; ev[e]=idx+1; nxt[e]=head[w]; head[w]=e; e+=1
    if i+1<H:
      w = F[idx] if F[idx] < F[idx+W] else F[idx+W]
      eu[e]=idx; ev[e]=idx+W; nxt[e]=head[w]; head[w]=e; e+=1
```
Then Kruskal:
```
for w in range(maxF, 0, -1):
    e = head[w]
    while e != -1:
        union(eu[e], ev[e], w)
        e = nxt[e]
```

Edge generation with buckets:
```
N = H*W

head = [-1]*(maxF+1)
E = 2*H*W - H - W  # number of undirected edges
eu = [0]*E
ev = [0]*E
nxt = [0]*E
e = 0
for i in range(H):
    base = i*W
    for j in range(W):
        idx = base + j
        fi = F_flat[idx]
        if j+1 < W:
            fj = F_flat[idx+1]
            w = fi if fi < fj else fj
            eu[e] = idx
            ev[e] = idx+1
            nxt[e] = head[w]
            head[w] = e
            e += 1
        if i+1 < H:
            fj = F_flat[idx+W]
            w = fi if fi < fj else fj
            eu[e] = idx
            ev[e] = idx+W
            nxt[e] = head[w]
            head[w] = e
            e += 1
```

Kruskal:
```
dsu = list(range(N))
size = [1]*N
root_node = list(range(N))
parent_up0 = [-1]*(2*N)
val = F_flat  # use same list
next_node = N

def find(x):
    while dsu[x] != x:
        dsu[x] = dsu[dsu[x]]
        x = dsu[x]
    return x

for w in range(maxF, 0, -1):
    e = head[w]
    while e != -1:
        u = eu[e]; v = ev[e]
        ra = find(u); rb = find(v)
        if ra != rb:
            if size[ra] < size[rb]:
                ra, rb = rb, ra
            new = next_node; next_node += 1
            val.append(w)
            ca = root_node[ra]
            cb = root_node[rb]
            parent_up0[ca] = new
            parent_up0[cb] = new
            dsu[rb] = ra
            size[ra] += size[rb]
            root_node[ra] = new
        e = nxt[e]
```
Need think: `val = F_flat`; after appending internal nodes, F_flat list grows. But we still need F_flat for edge generation? Edge generation already done before Kruskal. So okay. But if we use `val` for weights and also `F_flat` variable, no issue. After Kruskal, `val` length total_nodes.

Could we build up table before depth? Doesn't matter. Depth uses parent_up0. Up table uses parent_up0. We can build up first then depth? Depth computation uses parent_up0; if parent_up0 is up[0], okay. But up table list `up` holds parent_up0; no issue.

Now LCA function. For speed, avoid function call overhead? Q=2e5, function call okay but can inline? We'll define local variables for speed.

```
def lca(u, v, depth=depth, up=up, LOG=LOG):
    if depth[u] < depth[v]:
        u, v = v, u
    diff = depth[u] - depth[v]
    bit = 0
    while diff:
        if diff & 1:
            u = up[bit][u]
        diff >>= 1
        bit += 1
    if u == v:
        return u
    for k in range(LOG-1, -1, -1):
        uu = up[k][u]
        vv = up[k][v]
        if uu != vv:
            u = uu
            v = vv
    return up[0][u]
```
Need if u/v are leaf ids 0..N-1. In KRT, leaves are 0..N-1. Query cells map to id = (A-1)*W + (B-1). Good.

Then answer formula:
```
out = []
for _ in range(Q):
    A,B,Y,C,D,Z = map(int, input().split())
    s = (A-1)*W + (B-1)
    t = (C-1)*W + (D-1)
    m = lca(s,t)
    M = val[m]
    mn = Y if Y < Z else Z
    ans = abs(Y-Z) + 2 * max(0, mn - M)
    out.append(str(ans))
sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```
Need if s/t are leaves; yes.

Now, let's refine code:

```
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    H, W = map(int, input().split())
    N = H * W
    F = []
    for _ in range(H):
        F.extend(map(int, input().split()))
    maxF = max(F)
    E = 2*N - H - W
    head = [-1] * (maxF + 1)
    eu = [0] * E
    ev = [0] * E
    nxt = [0] * E
    e = 0
    for i in range(H):
        base = i * W
        for j in range(W):
            idx = base + j
            fi = F[idx]
            if j + 1 < W:
                fj = F[idx + 1]
                w = fi if fi < fj else fj
                eu[e] = idx
                ev[e] = idx + 1
                nxt[e] = head[w]
                head[w] = e
                e += 1
            if i + 1 < H:
                fj = F[idx + W]
                w = fi if fi < fj else fj
                eu[e] = idx
                ev[e] = idx + W
                nxt[e] = head[w]
                head[w] = e
                e += 1

    dsu = list(range(N))
    size = [1] * N
    root_node = list(range(N))
    parent_up0 = [-1] * (2 * N)
    val = F  # reuse list for KRT node values
    next_node = N

    def find(x, dsu=dsu):
        while dsu[x] != x:
            dsu[x] = dsu[dsu[x]]
            x = dsu[x]
        return x

    for w in range(maxF, 0, -1):
        e = head[w]
        while e != -1:
            u = eu[e]
            v = ev[e]
            ra = find(u)
            rb = find(v)
            if ra != rb:
                if size[ra] < size[rb]:
                    ra, rb = rb, ra
                new = next_node
                next_node += 1
                val.append(w)
                ca = root_node[ra]
                cb = root_node[rb]
                parent_up0[ca] = new
                parent_up0[cb] = new
                dsu[rb] = ra
                size[ra] += size[rb]
                root_node[ra] = new
            e = nxt[e]

    total_nodes = next_node
    root = total_nodes - 1
    parent_up0[root] = root
    parent_up0 = parent_up0[:total_nodes]

    del head, eu, ev, nxt, dsu, size, root_node, find

    depth = [0] * total_nodes
    for v in range(total_nodes - 1, -1, -1):
        if v != root:
            depth[v] = depth[parent_up0[v]] + 1

    LOG = total_nodes.bit_length()
    up = [parent_up0]
    for _ in range(1, LOG):
        prev = up[-1]
        curr = [0] * total_nodes
        for v in range(total_nodes):
            curr[v] = prev[prev[v]]
        up.append(curr)

    def lca(u, v, depth=depth, up=up, LOG=LOG):
        if depth[u] < depth[v]:
            u, v = v, u
        diff = depth[u] - depth[v]
        bit = 0
        while diff:
            if diff & 1:
                u = up[bit][u]
            diff >>= 1
            bit += 1
        if u == v:
            return u
        for k in range(LOG - 1, -1, -1):
            pu = up[k][u]
            pv = up[k][v]
            if pu != pv:
                u = pu
                v = pv
        return up[0][u]

    Q = int(input())
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        A, B, Y, C, D, Z = map(int, input().split())
        s = (A - 1) * W + (B - 1)
        t = (C - 1) * W + (D - 1)
        m = lca(s, t)
        M = val[m]
        mn = Y if Y < Z else Z
        ans = abs(Y - Z) + 2 * (mn - M if mn > M else 0)
        out.append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Either. We'll keep no trailing newline to avoid extra if Q=0? Q>=1. Both fine.
